| 2026-07-23 | **PR #102 (chart perf trio) MERGED after the double adversarial pass — 3 review fixes landed on main before deploy.** Founder's worktree agent shipped the three fixes; orchestrator reviewed line-by-line + two split-mandate Opus reviewers (backend merge-boundary/storm vs frontend persistence/staleness), both approve-with-nits. Real catches fixed pre-deploy: (1) **futures aggregated timeframes corrupted the merge boundary** — Schwab has no native 1Hour/4Hour/1Week/1Month; it aggregates 1Day/30Min in-process and the bucket STAMPS depend on the fetch window (weekly buckets stamp group[0].time = first day IN the window; hourly buckets aggregate only in-window 30Min bars), so an incremental window merged over a full baseline duplicated a week candle or downgraded an hour bucket's O/H/L/V. Guard: `_FUTURES_AGGREGATED_TIMEFRAMES` skip in `_fetch_bars_incremental`, futures-scoped (equities keep merging — Alpaca bars are natively calendar-anchored); +1 test proving both sides. (2) cap-trim log claimed "1Day coverage preserved" — false for 200+ name watchlists (baseline entries past 160 get cut); log now counts 1Day-baseline symbols left cold. (3) `parsePersisted` accepted any non-null `data` — a stale-schema sessionStorage blob (deploy changes shape, storage key not bumped) would rehydrate and throw `data.bars.map is not a function` in EVERY chart; the vitest fixture itself encoded the invalid shape (bars: string) — added `looksLikeBarsPayload` array guard + fixed fixture + rejection test. | 2026-07-23 | **Chart bars: reload-persistent frontend cache (chart-perf audit fix #3, same PR as #1/#2).** The frontend bars cache (`frontend/lib/bars-cache.ts`) was a plain in-memory Map, so a full reload (F5, restored tab) dropped every chart to cold even for still-within-TTL entries. Fix: mirror the cache into **sessionStorage** — rehydrate on module load, flush on tab-hide (`pagehide` + `visibilitychange→hidden`). Transparent to callers: `fetchBarsCached` still applies the TTL, so a rehydrated-but-expired entry refetches exactly as before (and the live-tick stale-tail guard from `chart-stale-merge-fix` still protects intraday) — the win is instant paint on a within-TTL reload with no round-trip. sessionStorage (not local) = survives reload/same-tab-nav, clears on tab close (no unbounded cross-session growth); all access best-effort + guarded (private-mode/quota → cold start). The tricky bits — newest-first byte-budget eviction (3.5MB, under the ~5MB quota) and corrupt-blob tolerance — are factored into a pure, dependency-free `frontend/lib/bars-cache-persist.ts` so they're node-testable without jsdom; +5 vitest tests (`bars-cache-persist.test.ts`, vitest `include` broadened one line, still node-env/no-jsdom). **57/57 vitest + typecheck clean.** Frontend-only; verify in-browser. Draft PR now carries all three audit fixes (#1 incremental refresh, #2 broadened prewarm, #3 this); deploy + founder verify pending. |
| 2026-07-23 | **Chart bars: broadened prewarm — watchlist kept warm across the timeframes the user actually uses (chart-perf audit fix #2, same PR as #1).** The prewarm loop warmed only the 1Day default view for watchlist symbols; every other timeframe was cold on first view (and only kept warm 30 min after being viewed, per-symbol). Fix (`api/main.py` `_bars_prewarm_once`): broadcast the K most-recently-used distinct `(timeframe, days)` specs (`_BARS_HOT_SPECS_MAX=2` — ~the timeframes the user is actively in) across the WHOLE watchlist, so switching symbols or switching to a timeframe on a not-yet-viewed symbol is served warm. This is exactly the seen-specs × symbols cross-product the 07-22 storm forbade — affordable now ONLY because fix #1 (incremental refresh) makes each warm refresh a tiny trailing-window fetch, not a full walk. Bounded two ways: K hot specs + a hard `_BARS_PREWARM_MAX_ENTRIES=160` backstop trimmed from the tail (default-1Day pushed first → baseline coverage never sacrificed; hot slots ordered by symbol view-recency, never a blind alphabetical SPY/TSLA clip per `flow-sub-cap-alphabetical-clip`; trims are `log()`-ed — no silent caps). Storm invariant unchanged: judge a full cold pass stays < the shortest TTL it maintains via `/health` bars_prewarm `lag_s`. Replaced the old "no cross-product" test pin with 3 (broadcast reaches all symbols; K-bound leaves older specs per-symbol-only; entry cap preserves 1Day + gives scarce hot slots to recent symbols + logs). 10/10 module + 53 adjacent green, ruff clean. K / MAX_ENTRIES tunable. Draft PR (with fix #1); deploy + founder verify pending. |
| 2026-07-23 | **Chart bars: incremental fetch-since-last-bar refresh — warm refreshes stop re-walking the whole range (chart-perf audit fix #1).** Root cause of "watchlist charts still slow on some timeframes": `_fetch_bars` re-fetched the ENTIRE `days` range on every TTL expiry, so keeping a series warm cost a full cold load — which is exactly why the prewarm loop's scope was pinned to the 1Day default only (broadening it outran the TTLs it refreshes → the 07-22 storm relapses). Fix (`api/main.py`): `_fetch_bars` now dispatches incremental-vs-full — a warm series with a fresh full baseline re-fetches only a small trailing window (`_BARS_INCREMENTAL_DAYS`, 2–90d by timeframe) and merges it over the cached bars (still-forming last bar overwritten, new bars appended; ascending same-provider ISO times keep the concat sorted+deduped). Full re-baseline forced hourly (`_BARS_FULL_REBASELINE=3600s`) so split adjustments (Alpaca `adjustment=split`) + upstream history revisions propagate instead of living on stale bars. Empty window / provider error / `inc_days>=days` → falls back to a full walk (which owns the Alpaca-fallback + stale-if-error resilience). Refactor extracted `_stockbars_to_dicts` / `_build_bars_response` / `_store_bars` (shared by both paths; `_BARS_FULL_AT` tracks last-full per key, evicted alongside `_BARS_CACHE`). ENABLER for fix #2 (prewarm more than the default timeframe) without re-triggering the storm. `tests/test_bars_cache.py` +3 (merge/boundary-replace, hourly rebaseline, empty→full fallback); 8/8 module + 53 adjacent (intraday/postmarket/overnight/scan/research/trade-context) green; ruff clean. Draft PR from worktree; deploy + founder verify pending. |

| 2026-07-23 | **Terminal chat pop round (founder: the chat "blends in… I'd like it brighter on a darker theme or darker on a brighter theme, so it pops out and is really the focus point — I don't want it to blur anything behind it" + board card fr-1783787088 wall-of-text/timestamps).** Root cause of the blend-in: the open drawer painted `--bg-raised`/90 + backdrop-blur — the same token as every toolbar, translucent, plus exactly the blur he doesn't want; the chat widget was fully transparent on the generic `--panel` shell. Fix = **dedicated terminal surface tokens** (`--terminal-surface`, `--terminal-surface-focus`) built the md-paper way: `color-mix` toward `--text-primary`, which moves toward CONTRAST in both directions — brighter on carbon/ember/oled, inked-darker on light — so ONE formula tracks every theme including customs (no per-theme table to maintain, theme editor can still override later). Open drawer = solid focus surface + `shadow-xl` + strong border (zero translucency/blur); chat widget sits on the base surface and **lights up one step on focus-within** (typing = lit). **Timestamps** (the card's second ask, for back-checking what price a turn referenced): user rows show HH:MM (older: "Jul 22 14:03", browser-local) — stamped at the chat runtime's `onFinish` so ONLY turns that actually ran live get times; server-restored history renders NO stamp rather than a fabricated load-time (honesty rule applied to UI chrome); stamps persist in localStorage (cap 600, first-stamp-wins so approval-resume re-finishes can't restamp). **Wall-of-text relief**: hairline + breathing room at every turn boundary chunk the transcript into turns. Typecheck green, deployed 16:12Z. Pending founder in-browser check; deeper info-design pass reopens on his word. |

Also learned: AV rows carry no source key → upsert tags them 'finnhub', so 'finnhub' = "vendor estimate" and the confirmed set must union ALL vendor fetches. Built: `prune_withdrawn_estimates` (db/earnings_calendar.py) wired into arefresh only — future rows die when unconfirmed; day-of rows NEVER touched; past rows die only if abandoned ≥3d before the event (real dates are restamped through the event day; edgar rows untouchable); prune skips entirely unless every configured vendor fully succeeded. Opus adversarial review reproduced 2 real holes, both folded pre-commit: (1) Finnhub empty-but-200 (dead key) wasn't a failure → empty confirmed set would have wiped the forward calendar; (2) AV is FORWARD-ONLY — it drops a date the moment it becomes today, so a real AMC date could be pruned ON its print day and blind the exit engine (the day-of + 3d-margin rules exist because of this). Plus review hardenings: normalized confirmed pairs, blast-radius cap (abort >60% — first-run backlog measured 48%, steady state ~1%). 9 new tests; full suite 2,572 green. ⚠ Self-inflicted footnote: the queued 10:06:30 deploy restarted the backend mid-edit → the boot refresh crashed (tuple-unpack) every 24h-iteration; this round's deploy ships the complete code. DEPLOYED 14:28Z + **the cap fired on its FIRST live run** (AV returned a stub CSV — 3rd call of the day — 809/926 read as withdrawn → correctly kept everything); backlog healed by a one-time SQL pass using last night's fully-verified fetch as the confirmed set (454 deleted, dry-run first; BEN kept real 07-31, ABBV kept its double-filer 07-31, GOOGL left with only the real 07-22). Founder: one-time sudo to install the unit. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Two price-honesty bugs, both found from one founder question about NTSK (branch `worktree-fix+idea-tracker-post-close-grading`).** Founder asked why an idea outcome "just shows price" under resolved, then: *"why the hell does it say -10% and 12.78 when the chart shows 14.50 at close. lack of bids sitting in the order book?"* Both answers were bugs, and **his order-book instinct was right about where to look**. **(1) The overnight quote was a ghost.** At 20:14 ET the chart + watchlist showed NTSK **12.78 / -10.44%** on a stock that had **closed 14.27, +11% on the day** (real post-market print 14.4448 @ 19:52 ET, bid 14.41/ask 14.45 — a healthy book, so *not* a liquidity vacuum). Past 20:00 ET `market_session()=='overnight'`, so `HybridMarketData.aget_quotes` routes equity ext-quotes to Alpaca's Blue Ocean feed (`boats`). NTSK has **no overnight market** — and the snapshot serves the last trade it *ever* saw: a **100-share print from the PRIOR overnight session, 07-13 23:35 ET, 20.6h stale**, superseded by an entire +11% regular session. `_parse_ext_quote_snapshot` read `latestTrade.p` and **never looked at `latestTrade.t`** — no staleness guard anywhere on that path; the -10.44% is then computed *correctly* from a bad price, which is why it looked plausible. Measured live, same instant: `feed=boats -> 12.78` vs `feed=sip -> 14.4448`. This is a gap in the shipped `overnight-24-5-boats` work and the **third** member of a family already scarred into `schwab_market_data.py` (stale `extended` block; echo-close phantom) — and since `ext_price` feeds `_poll_ext_quotes_for_alerts`, a ghost price is a **phantom-alert source**, not just a cosmetic one. Fix: `trading/market_hours.last_session_close(now)` + an opt-in `not_before` guard — drop any ext quote whose last trade predates the last regular close — and generalize hybrid's existing **all-or-nothing** fallback (`if not ext`) to **per-symbol** (a mixed watchlist has some names trading overnight and some not; the batch test never fired). Daytime/pre/post paths unchanged (guard is opt-in). **Verified live: ext_last 12.78 -> 14.4448, ext_change_pct -10.44 -> +1.22.** **(2) The idea tracker graded an idea against its own pre-history.** NTSK was marked `lost` **four minutes after it was created**. The nightly scan ran 17:44 ET on that same +11% breakout day (open 12.48 / **low 12.40** / close 14.27) and proposed the 14.23-14.37 zone, stop 13.59 — then the sweep replayed the **same already-complete bar**, saw the 12.40 *morning* low under the stop, and stopped it out. **The stop-out predates the breakout the idea was built on.** `_process_pending` skipped only `bar_date < as_of_date`, and `as_of_date` was the **UTC** date of a post-close timestamp, so the birth-day bar always counted. **Every real idea is minted post-close** (scans run after the bell — all 8 rows in the ledger), so this contaminates the whole calibration ledger and it **fabricates LOSSES**, deflating win% — the opposite direction from the same-bar rules the file already guards against, and invisible because the resolved row shows only a price. Fix: `_first_evaluable_date()` — an idea minted after the close grades from the **next trading day** (bar filter + fetch bound + TTL basis, keeping `fetch_daily_bars_since`'s load-bearing tri-state: the evening of the scan now returns `[]` = "no session yet", not a resolvable bar). Intraday ideas untouched — all 28 pre-existing tests still pass. **Verify:** 61 targeted + 142 related tests green exit 0, ruff clean repo-wide; both new suites are **true repros** — reverted, `test_post_close_idea_is_not_graded_by_its_own_birth_bar` fails `assert 1 == 0` (it *was* marked lost) and the per-symbol fallback test shows only the stale `boats` call. DST probed directly: spring-forward resolves EST(-5), fall-back EDT(-4), 16:00:00 counts as closed. ⚠ **Data repair still owed**: `idea_outcomes` id=8 and id=10 (NTSK, artifacts 183/184) are fabricated losses — reset or discard before they reach win%; the fix stops new ones but does not heal these. ⚠ **Founder's actual UI ask is still open**: resolved rows show `resolution_price` alone with **three different meanings** under one header (planned stop on the pending path, actual bar low on the triggered path, planned target on wins) and no entry/stop/plan context — every field is already in the API payload (`list_recent_resolved` is `SELECT *`), so it is a UI-layer gap only. Also seen: 183/184 are **duplicate** NTSK ideas (same plan, two artifacts ~19 min apart). Follow-up: hybrid's broad `except Exception` swallowed a `TypeError` from the new kwarg — it hides signature errors. |

| 2026-07-14 | **Data-layer limits audit + THE root data bug: the flow engine ran on 0.7% of the chain (branch `fix/flow-chain-pool-truncation`).** Founder: *"what are our Alpaca rate limits, what are our system limits, why are they in place… we need solid architecture here. this is the data. without good data this app is nothing."* Full audit → `References/DATA_LAYER_LIMITS_AUDIT_2026-07-14.md`. **Alpaca is not the bottleneck anywhere**: we use ~30 of **10,000** REST req/min (0.3%) on Algo Trader Plus, and 2.76% of one core on the fattest feed they sell. The only hard vendor wall is `*` forbidden on option *quotes*; 1 ws connection **per endpoint** (SIP/OPRA independent); "unlimited" websocket symbols on our tier; the real ceiling is the reactive `407 slow client`, not any cap. **Two of our beliefs were false**: (a) the "Alpaca caps option quotes at 1,000/connection" claim in `FLOW_CAPACITY_RESEARCH_2026-07-11.md:24` **does not exist in any Alpaca doc** — it's *Polygon's* cap, imported by mistake, and our own `ws_probe_2026-07-09.md` had already refuted it the day it was written (1,506 subs ACKed, *"treat 1,000 as our own budget, not a vendor wall"*); corrected in-file this round. (b) `HOT_QUOTE_MAX=60` is **stale, not baseless** — the 07-09 soak (150 quote subs ≈ 2,100 msg/s ≈ **52% of a core**, `0471d7b`) was a real live measurement; it just predates the 07-12 rewrite (`0de5205`) that made the hot path 3.1× faster and has never been re-run (founder's recall of this was better than the audit's first pass). **The actual bug isn't a cap at all**: `poll.py` called `get_option_chain_snapshot(u)` **bare** → `limit=None, max_pages=1` → Alpaca's default **100 contracts, alphabetical**. An OCC sorts expiry → **C before P** → strike, so one bare page = the nearest expiry, **calls only**. Measured live: **SPY has 13,730 contracts / 35 expiries / 6,865 puts and we saw 100 calls at one expiry — 0.7% of the chain**; **11 of 30 underlyings never saw a put** (`AMD,CRWD,GLD,GS,KLAC,MU,NBIS,QQQ,SPCX,SPY,TSLA` — it hits the *most liquid* names hardest, since more strikes means the page fills with calls before reaching `P`); 22 of 30 saw one expiry. Since `aggregate_directional_pressure` tracks `call_net`/`put_net`, **`put_net` was structurally zero for SPY/QQQ/TSLA — every directional read on them was bullish by construction, a signal fabricated by omission.** `ACTIVE_SET_PER_UNDERLYING=60` never once selected on merit (it picked 60 from a pre-truncated alphabetical 100), which means every cap debated earlier today sat downstream of a gutted pool. ⚠️ **We already knew**: BUILD.md 2026-07-09 records the identical diagnosis (*"unfiltered snapshot pages alphabetically → one expiry, no puts"*) — found and fixed **in the chain viewer only**; the poll was never carried across, and the method's docstring enshrined it (*"Defaults… keep the original single-page behavior the poll's active-set relies on"*). The UI the founder reads manually was correct; the engine meant to think for him was not. Fix: page the chain (`CHAIN_SNAPSHOT_PAGE_LIMIT=1000` vendor max, `CHAIN_SNAPSHOT_MAX_PAGES=20`; SPY = 14 pages in 1.16s, whole watchlist ≈16s per 300s refresh, ~0.3% of budget) + the pager now **warns** on truncation instead of dropping the tail silently. **Verified against the LIVE chain**: active set = 1,000 contracts, **395 puts / 605 calls**, 30 underlyings, **29 distinct expiries**; **SPY now 20 puts / 16 calls (was 0 puts)**. 196 options_flow tests green, ruff clean; the new test is a true behavioural repro (old code fails with *"one page IS the vendor's alphabetical default — no puts"*). Audit's standing rule: **every limit names its owner (vendor citation or a measurement on current code), logs loudly when it binds, and ranks survival by importance — never `sorted()`**; all four defects of 07-13/14 violated exactly that, and hit TSLA every time because `T` sorts late. ⚠ Consequence flagged: ~1,800 contracts now rank active vs the 1,000 sub budget, so `STREAM_TRADES_MAX` binds hard for the first time (~33-36/underlying) — vendor acked 1,506 and the client has ~150× headroom so 1000→1500 is *probably* free, but unverified. Open gaps: a live quote soak on post-rewrite code (the #1 gap — every probe since 07-09 subscribes `trades:['*']` only, and quotes run ~20:1; prod's "7,400 msg/s" is circular since `HOT_QUOTE_MAX` suppresses the very volume in question), a push past 1,506 subs, branch W/C at a real open with a capped set, and standing CPU telemetry. Landed after the close (multiplies the candidate pool ~50×). |

Two stacked causes: `_refresh_active_set` ranks by `latestTrade` **today**, which at the bell is empty by construction (`state.oi` was only ever used to enrich, never to seed membership); and `SUB_REFRESH_SECONDS=300` is anchored to **connect** time, not the market clock (the only forced refresh runs ~15 min pre-open when the session is still `pre` ⇒ empty set), so the first post-open re-check landed 0–5 min late and drifted daily. UI: workspace word expands upward into a 560px two-pane manager (workspaces | pages-in-ws + library w/ usage tags, add/reorder/rename/remove/delete-everywhere); `+ pg` becomes Blank/From-library menu; pill context menu gains Detach-a-copy/Remove-from-workspace/Delete-everywhere; one-time SharedPageNotice strip above the input bar on shared pages. | 2026-07-13 | **Watchlist round 13 (branch `fix/grain-above`): the midnight-oil notch/lines — grain-theme root cause, pixel-proven.** Founder: notch persisted (Edge + Firefox) AND new heavy line segments under the symbol column; then the decisive datapoint — 'i don't see the issue on carbon, oled, light' = the NO-GRAIN themes. Mechanism (measured: symbol column = #121212+8% noise, rest of row = flat #121212): the widget's film grain (.grain-surface::before) sits at z-0, so every positioned element — rows, hover/selected fills, opaque sticky cells — paints OVER it, leaving flat patches; the round-3 fix re-added grain INSIDE the sticky cells only, so grain themes rendered two mismatched textures side by side (the 'notch' = flat 4px padding beside the grained cell; the 'lines' = texture step at the cell boundary next to dividers). Fix: new .grain-above (::before z-index 3) on WidgetCard — grain paints ONCE above the entire widget body (under all overlays: settings z-10, popovers z-50; the CANVAS grain-surface stays z-0 so widgets aren't double-grained) — and all three per-cell .grain-overlay patches deleted (class removed from globals). The prior select-none fix stays (text-selection wash was real, proven via selectNodeContents). Verified: tsc exit 0; pixel runs show uniform noise across symbol + data areas, hovered + selected fills flush edge-to-edge, dividers single-weight, on both the desktop workspace and the narrow mobile shell. |

Forensic chain: pixel colors ≠ every computed style (elementsFromPoint, pseudo-elements, inline-style burst all clean), blue transient across identical probe runs → finally reproduced by programmatically selecting a row's contents: a click with a 1px pointer wobble TEXT-SELECTS the row, and Chromium paints its native selection wash over the content boxes but NOT the row padding — bright blue band + un-tinted notch at the left, on any theme. (The reorder code already called removeAllRanges() on drag start — known hazard, half-mitigated.) Fix: Reorder.Group rows are select-none unconditionally (was drag-only); rows are controls (click = publish, drag = reorder), text selection has no job. | 2026-07-12 | **Board knock-out session (branch `worktree-board-knockout`, draft PR): settings RMW race + strip-page bottom padding.** (1) **`settings-rmw-race` (safety-adjacent):** `apply_settings_patch()` did `load_settings()` (locks/unlocks) → re-read on-disk → merge → `save_settings()` (locks/unlocks) with the lock RELEASED between read and write, so two concurrent callers last-writer-win — a PUT touching an unrelated field could lost-update a breaker-armed `kill_switch_active`/`entries_halted` back to False. Fix: the whole RMW now runs under a NEW `_patch_lock` (via a `_apply_settings_patch_locked` helper) — deliberately **separate** from `_settings_lock`, which stays a plain `Lock()` held only briefly by `load_settings`/`save_settings`. Adversarial review (Sonnet) caught the naive "just widen `_settings_lock` to an RLock" first pass as a real regression: an `"ai"` patch calls `maybe_upgrade_default_llm` → a synchronous model-list HTTP probe (up to ~6s × several endpoints × configured providers), and holding that under the reader lock would stall `is_kill_switch_active`/`is_entries_halted` (which gate every order in `risk_engine`) for seconds. With `_patch_lock`, a slow probe only serializes patch-vs-patch (rare) and never blocks the fast readers. Verified no lock-ordering hazard (functions inside the section — `get_watchlists_state`, `maybe_upgrade_default_llm`, `sync_default_llm_fields`, `save_settings` I/O — take no other lock). Deterministic regression test stalls thread A mid-RMW while thread B arms the kill switch and asserts the arm survives — **proven to FAIL on the old code (`assert False is True`) and pass on the fix**; 86 settings/safety/auth/research/strategy-scan tests green, ruff clean. (2) **`strip-padding-fullbleed` (UI):** `/board` + `/research` scroll content now reserve `calc(1.5rem + 40px)` bottom padding (`TERMINAL_COLLAPSED_HEIGHT`) so the last cards/content aren't hidden under the fixed terminal strip overlay — mirrors what the workspace page already does; tsc clean, pending in-browser check. |

| 2026-07-08 | **Mobile watchlist feel: stop press-feedback firing on scroll.** Founder: rows were "haptic happy" + felt like "almost pushing the stock" every scroll. Cause: `PressableScale` fires its haptic + scale-down on `onPressIn` (touch-down), which triggers on every scroll-start before the scroll gesture is recognized. Fix: `unstable_pressDelay={70}` on the row (`RowBody`) + 2-col card (`WatchlistCard`) PressableScales — a finger that moves (scroll) cancels the press before 70ms so no haptic/scale fires, while a deliberate tap still registers. Value dialed in with the founder (120→80→60→**70**). Note: drag-to-reorder long-press now starts ~70ms later (pressDelay + delayLongPress 250); acceptable. tsc clean. |

(1) **Truncation, both surfaces:** `run_chat_loop`'s `max_tokens` default was **1200** (`loop.py:229`) and NEITHER web nor mobile overrode it → DeepSeek hit `finish_reason=length` mid-report (tables/multi-tool synthesis exceed 1200). Fix: `_RESOURCE_ALIASES` in `ai/perception/resources.py::query` maps the natural guesses → real keys (`market_regime`/`regime`→`market`, plus `trades`→`my_trades`, `alerts`→`price_alerts`, `journal`→`decision_journal`, `accounts`→`schwab_accounts`) so any model's reasonable guess resolves. | 2026-07-07 | **Red CI made green — 5 real pre-existing pytest failures fixed (the source of the founder's GitHub failure emails).** CI had been red on essentially every push; the actual `python -m pytest -q` failures (verified from the Actions job log, not assumed): (1–4) **`test_regime_sleeves` — a genuine fixed-date staleness bug**: `_scan_vol_event` gated the `term_flip` trigger on `vix3m_is_fresh(db_path=...)` WITHOUT threading the scan's `now`, so the freshness check fell back to wall-clock `datetime.now()` while the tests inject `now=2026-07-03`; as real time advanced past the seeded ~07-02 vix3m data it read "stale" → trigger went dormant → zero proposals → 4 deterministic failures (local + CI). | 2026-07-04 | **Grid slide/overlap on page switch FIXED + founder-verified (founder card "copilot-grid-shift"), deployed.** Root cause was NOT the docked-copilot paddingRight (already excluded from measurement): the workspace grid's `cols`/`width` were React state recomputed a frame LATE (rAF effect), and react-grid-layout v2 remounts per page (`key=activePageId`) — so hot-swapping to a wide page (3 charts ≫ viewport cols) mounted RGL against the PREVIOUS page's col count, its `correctBounds` clamped right-side widgets leftward onto their neighbors (no compaction), the late cols correction never re-syncs RGL's internal state, and the next drag-stop PERSISTED the clamped positions wholesale via `persistLayout`'s merge. Fix (`workspace-grid.tsx`, hand-edit, −16 net lines): `cols`/`width` are now pure derived values from `(layout, hostWidth)` computed in render — the same render that hands RGL a page's layout always hands it that layout's col count; only the ResizeObserver-measured host width stays in state. Kills the whole one-frame-stale-cols bug class. Also this session: committed the parked working-tree round (board seen-marker UTC/monotonic fixes, custom-theme server-clobber guard, 5 sleeve strategy docs) + relearned the ops rule the hard way — `npm run build` under the live `next start` broke `/` until the frontend restart (rule now: build only as part of an immediate deploy). Two-round adversarial Sonnet review (SAFE TO COMMIT): caught + fixed off-hours-stale-price and Alpaca-daily-bar staleness (→ RTH gate + intraday 5-min bars + a today-date guard for the at-the-open window). **850 tests** green, ruff clean; live-verified `_live_market_price("SPY")` returns a price on a hub miss. | 2026-06-25 | **CPU peg fixed — missing index on a poll-loop analytics query (101% → 2%).** A single core was pinned 24/7. Diagnosis took several wrong turns (the first instinct — "we re-fetch a year of bars every poll" — was wrong; verified daily `days=12` returns 8 bars not 252, and two speculative caches — sub-daily `bar_cache` + a short-TTL endpoint `response_cache` — moved CPU 0%, so both were reverted). A py-spy thread dump caught the real culprit: ONE thread spinning in `compute_and_save_snapshot_analytics` → `list_equity_history` (`db/sqlite.py`). That fires every 20s (poll loop, default account) and runs a 7-day equity JOIN of `poll_runs ⋈ account_snapshots`; `account_snapshots(poll_run_id)` had **no index**, so the JOIN was an O(n·m) nested-loop full-scan that grew with the DB — measured **43,623 ms** at 35k rows, overlapping every 20s into a permanent pool-saturating peg. Fix = one index: `idx_account_snapshots_poll_run ON account_snapshots(poll_run_id)` (+ `idx_position_snapshots_poll_run` for parity on the 86k-row table), added to `init_db` (idempotent, created on startup). Same query **43,623 ms → 18 ms (2,400×)**; live backend CPU **101.7% → 2.1%**. No `trading/` touch (snapshot/analytics read path only); 817 tests. **Lesson: profile before caching — every cache here was solving a non-problem; the bug was an unindexed FK join.** (The frontend's universe-sweep fetch storm the recon flagged is a separate, lower-priority I/O item, not the CPU peg.) |

| 2026-06-24 | **Live WS streaming actually works (dropped-tick root cause) + price-alert delivery + 10 Hz throttle — the three Mac/RN serv items.** Root cause found by a live-stream probe: the Schwab WS never streamed a single tick to clients — the phone always fell back to the ~6-12s REST crawl. The stream was connected and delivering ~1 tick/sec/symbol, but `SchwabMarketStreamHub._on_equity`/`_on_futures` read each tick's fields by the numeric enum `.value` (`LAST_PRICE.value`=3) while **schwab-py relabels every content dict to the field MEMBER NAME** (`"LAST_PRICE"`) before the handler runs (`_Handler.label_message`). So `item.get(3)` was always `None` → `price is None → continue` → **zero broadcasts, ever** (symbol came through the literal `"key"` field, so it looked "connected"). Fix: read by `.name` (`_EQ`/`_FUT` constants); verified against the LIVE stream (AAPL 297.38/+1.05%, NVDA 201.36, SPY 739.52 populate). **Streaming frame** now carries `ext_price`/`ext_change_pct`/`market_session` (`QuoteState` + `_quote_payload`); `BaseMarketHub.apply_extended()` lets the REST watchlist poll write ext-hours pricing through to the hub so every broadcast rides with it. **Price alerts** now broadcast FLAT (matching the rule-alert shape — was nested under `alert:{}`, which rendered blank on the phone) and ALSO fire an **APNs push** (`send_alert_push` gained a `data=` arg → flat `type:"price_alert"`/`symbol`/`alert_id` custom keys for tap-to-deep-link). **WS broadcast throttle** (`BROADCAST_THROTTLE_MS=100`): coalesces hot ticks to ~10 Hz/symbol/client (first tick immediate, further ticks collapse into one trailing send that re-reads state — latest price wins), alerts unthrottled — keeps the phone battery sane under Schwab's 30-100 ticks/sec. Also hardened the sync handler (deprecated `get_event_loop()` → `get_running_loop()` + `_inflight_ticks` hard-ref set so the GC can't cancel a broadcast mid-run). New tests: `tests/test_schwab_stream_handler.py` (4, labeled-key regression lock) + `tests/test_price_alert_dispatch.py` (5, flat alert + push + apply_extended + throttle). 725 tests, ruff + frontend typecheck clean. Mac/RN relay: `References/RN_STREAMING_ALERTS_RELAY.md`. ⚠️ Schwab allows ONE stream session per login — a local probe transiently kicks prod (reconnects ~2s). Schwab stays READ-ONLY. |

Nightly `_volume_profile_refresh_loop` (boot + 24h) builds profiles for all watchlist symbols. Frontend: `RvolInfo`/`WatchlistQuote.rvol` (`api.ts`), a fixed WS-tick merge bug that had dropped `rvol`, and a new **RVOL column** (`2.3×` + color band <1.5 normal/1.5–2 yellow/2–3 amber/>3 red, muted overnight, `—` warming). `MARKET_DATA_PROVIDER=schwab` flipped in `.env` (data path only; `BROKER` untouched). **Verified live end-to-end through the HTTP endpoint** on all 15 watchlist symbols (SPY 66.8M real consolidated vol; CAT 1.78×, SPY 1.23×, NVDA 0.83×, XLF 0.70× — calibrated, self-consistent). 671 tests, ruff + typecheck clean.
